Yeah… I think so. Not like happy in a normal “good mood” way, exactly, but earlier this month I felt kind of electric—really upbeat, like everything was suddenly possible.

I don’t know if that makes sense, or if I’m overreacting, but it felt like maybe I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually happy or excited lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not like happy in a normal “good mood” way, exactly, but earlier this month I felt kind of electric—really upbeat, like everything was suddenly possible.\n\nAnd then, um… it would flip pretty fast. I’d get irritated over tiny things, like if a contractor was delayed or my sister didn’t respond the way I expected, I’d snap more than I should have. I’m not proud of that. It just felt in the moment like they were blocking me, even though I know that probably sounds a bit much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're capable of accomplishing more than you normally can?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d put it that strongly. Maybe a little? It felt more like I was motivated and had a lot of ideas, not like I thought I could do anything impossible.\n\nI mean, I did feel unusually confident, I guess, but I’m not sure where the line is between being productive and… being unrealistic. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I mean, I’m still getting some sleep, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is… but lately I’ll either lie there replaying everything I think I messed up, or I wake up around four in the morning because of the traffic outside and then my brain just won’t quiet down again.\n\nI’m not sure if that counts as “trouble sleeping,” but it’s been happening enough that I’ve noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble focusing on one thing for a long time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think it’s been a huge problem, exactly. I mean, with design work I’m used to switching between things, so it’s hard to tell what’s normal for me.\n\nMaybe I’ve been a little more scattered at times, but I can still get things done if I really sit myself down. Is that the kind of focusing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I mean, I know some of it is probably just me being hard on myself, but I keep going over things and thinking I ruined the separation, or wasted money I shouldn’t have, or let clients down.\n\nAnd then it kind of turns into this bigger thing, like every practical problem somehow got worse because of me… because of who I am, I guess. That sounds dramatic when I say it out loud, sorry. But yes, I’ve been feeling guilty.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, because it’s not like I’m crying nonstop or anything, but lately I wake up with this kind of heavy, gray feeling in my chest.\n\nAnd even if the apartment is quiet and nothing is actually wrong in that moment, I’ll feel like I could cry over something stupid, like a coffee mug left in the sink. I know that sounds silly, but… yeah, it’s been there a lot of the day.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these mood swings?\n\nPatient: I guess… about a month? It just felt in the moment like they were blocking me, even though I know that probably sounds a bit much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're capable of accomplishing more than you normally can?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d put it that strongly. Maybe a little? It felt more like I was motivated and had a lot of ideas, not like I thought I could do anything impossible.\n\nI mean, I did feel unusually confident, I guess, but I’m not sure where the line is between being productive and… being unrealistic. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I mean, I’m still getting some sleep, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is… but lately I’ll either lie there replaying everything I think I messed up, or I wake up around four in the morning because of the traffic outside and then my brain just won’t quiet down again.\n\nI’m not sure if that counts as “trouble sleeping,” but it’s been happening enough that I’ve noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble focusing on one thing for a long time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think it’s been a huge problem, exactly. I mean, with design work I’m used to switching between things, so it’s hard to tell what’s normal for me.\n\nMaybe I’ve been a little more scattered at times, but I can still get things done if I really sit myself down. Is that the kind of focusing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I mean, I know some of it is probably just me being hard on myself, but I keep going over things and thinking I ruined the separation, or wasted money I shouldn’t have, or let clients down.\n\nAnd then it kind of turns into this bigger thing, like every practical problem somehow got worse because of me… because of who I am, I guess. That sounds dramatic when I say it out loud, sorry. But yes, I’ve been feeling guilty.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, because it’s not like I’m crying nonstop or anything, but lately I wake up with this kind of heavy, gray feeling in my chest.\n\nAnd even if the apartment is quiet and nothing is actually wrong in that moment, I’ll feel like I could cry over something stupid, like a coffee mug left in the sink. I know that sounds silly, but… yeah, it’s been there a lot of the day.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these mood swings?\n\nPatient: I guess… about a month? It really started feeling like this intense up-and-down thing after I moved into the apartment.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the right way to measure it, but since then it’s been more noticeable. Before that, I had normal stressful days, but this pattern has felt more… consistent since the move.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't need much sleep and still felt rested?\n\nPatient: Um… yes, I think so. Not all the time, but there were several nights where I only slept maybe three or four hours, and I’d wake up feeling like I was fine—like, weirdly rested enough.\n\nI kind of told myself it was productive, like I should use the extra hours to get ahead on things. I know that probably doesn’t sound great, but at the time it felt… reasonable? Like I didn’t feel exhausted the way I normally would.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
